图13-13-7 2号样品中红色颜料的EDS测试结果


为苯环上  $\nu_{\mathrm{C - O}}$  ，在  $1620\pm 2\mathrm{cm}^{-1}$  处的峰为烯烃碳碳双键的伸缩振动吸收峰  $\nu_{\mathrm{C = C}}$  。另外，红外吸收峰  $2924\mathrm{cm}^{-1}$  和  $2855\mathrm{cm}^{-1}$  分别属于亚甲基（  $\mathrm{CH}_2-$  ）的不对称伸缩振动峰  $\nu$  as和对称伸缩振动  $\nu_{\mathrm{s}}$  峰，在  $1400\mathrm{cm}^{-1}$  附近也可以观察到它的变形振动峰  $\delta$  。在  $1071\pm 3\mathrm{cm}^{-1}$  处出现的强峰为碳氧键的伸缩振动  $\nu_{\mathrm{C - O}}$  ，在  $1710\pm 3\mathrm{cm}^{-1}$  处的强峰为  $\nu_{\mathrm{C = O}}$  。

# 3. 电镜一能谱分析

对2号样品的红色颜料进行能谱检测，发现含有O、Si、Al、Mg、Hg、S等元素。（图13-13-7）

根据背反射电子原理，2号样品的红色颜料SEM图中可以看到原子衬度图像（原子序数越高，图像越亮），因此高光区域主要为  $\mathrm{Hg}$  和S，而EDX图中还能看到该区域存在少量的Al、Si等来自土壤的基本元素，说明红色颜料可能是朱砂，同时伴随少量的二氧化硅。

# （四）结论

通过显微镜观察并结合红外光谱和电镜能谱分析，可以得知1号样品为漆膜，推测黑陶杯为调制生漆的用具。2号样品中的红色颜料为朱砂，胶结材料为大漆，推测漆器采用了丹漆工艺。

# 后记

良渚遗址1936年发现，1959年正式命名良渚文化。1961年被公布为浙江省重点文物保护单位。1979年浙江省文物考古研究所正式成立，1981年发掘了余杭瓶窑吴家埠遗址，并在当地建立了工作站，从此良渚一带开始有了长期稳定的考古工作。

1994年，良渚遗址因其在中华文明起源阶段无与伦比的重要价值和保存的完整性，被国家文物局列入中国申报《世界遗产名录》的预备清单。

1996年，国务院批准良渚遗址（群）为全国重点文物保护单位。划定了东起良渚镇、西到瓶窑镇、北到天目山支脉的山边、南到良渚港的33.8平方千米的保护范围。

2001年9月，浙江省人民政府批准设立了杭州良渚遗址管理区，范围为扩并后的良渚、瓶窑两镇，区域面积242平方千米，成立了正区级的遗址保护专门机构——良渚遗址管理区管委会，加强了良渚遗址的保护管理力度。

2001年12月，杭州市人大审议通过了《杭州市良渚遗址保护管理条例》，使良渚遗址保护有了专门的针对性法规。

2013年，《良渚遗址总体保护规划》得到批准实施，良渚遗址的红线保护范围由原来的33.8平方千米调整为42.4平方千米。余杭区正式启动了良渚古城申报世界文化遗产的计划，目前申遗计划正在紧锣密鼓的实施之中。良渚古城所在的3平方千米范围内的住户已经在2013年年底搬迁完毕。

时光飞逝，五千年一瞬，回首间我也已经在良渚这片土地上发掘与寻觅了30年。作为一个考古工作者，我有幸亲历了许多重要的考古发现，看到良渚遗址发现、认知与保护的整个历程。历经五千年的风风雨雨，良渚古城的基本地貌能够保存至今，有着许多的偶然与必然。当四千年前的大洪水过后，这里曾经的辉煌便销声匿迹了。由于江南地区一直是水路交通，所以当年的莫角山宫殿区的高台成为杂树丛生的荒山，而城墙与那些临水的台地成为后来村庄的选址。20世纪50年代，莫角山成为杭州市民政局的果园，因此避免了70年代以后农村扩展开发的破坏。自1981年以后，由于浙江省文物考古研究所在长期不断的考古工作，良渚一带越来越多的遗址被发现，良渚遗址的保护也越来越受到各级政府的重视。特别是2001年良渚遗址管理区管委会成立以后，使良渚遗址得到了更加有效的管理和保护。

2007年良渚古城发现以后，在国家文物局和浙江省文物局的支持下，良渚遗址的考古工作

成为每年按计划进行的一项常规性工作。经过连续十年的考古勘探、调查与发掘，我们基本搞清楚了城内外的遗址分布与水系，初步确认了外郭城与外围水利系统，对城内莫角山宫殿区的堆筑情况以及遗迹分布情况也有了基本的了解。

2009年良渚遗址被列为第一批国家考古遗址公园，在国家文物局和浙江省文物局的关心支持下，浙江省文物考古研究所与良渚遗址管理区管委会合作成立了“良渚遗址考古与保护中心”，使良渚遗址的考古与保护工作得到更加有效与稳固的推进。

此外，良渚遗址的考古也是中华文明探源工程的重点项目之一。良渚古城发现之后，良渚、陶寺与二里头遗址成为探源工程中的三大都邑性课题。

在良渚遗址的考古中，我们与陕西龙腾勘探有限公司合作，已对古城及周边30多平方千米的范围进行了详细勘探，除了解古城遗址分布与结构外，还新发现了100余处遗址，良渚遗址100平方千米范围内已确认遗址点300多处。与西安十月科技有限公司等单位合作，对良渚古城所在的约120平方千米范围进行了航拍和测绘，并长期跟踪考古勘探与发掘进行数据采集，为考古地理信息系统的建立奠定了基础。我们与北京大学、南京大学、浙江大学、山东大学、华东师范大学、中国社会科学院考古研究所、中国丝绸博物馆以及日本金泽大学、奈良文化财研究所、京都大学、北海道大学、奈良教育大学等多家单位合作开展了多学科研究。在年代学、动物、植物、矿物、土壤、气候环境等方面取得了丰硕成果，对于理解良渚人的环境、食谱以及城墙土石来源等问题提供了科学依据。

本报告的写作历时两年，许多人为报告的编写付出了艰辛的劳动。希望尽可能全面地反映我们历年来的工作，全面解读我们目前关于良渚文化与良渚古城的认识。考虑到本报告作为申遗的资料性读本，因此，在体例上并未完全按照考古报告的通行模式，而是尽可能在科学的基础上进行通俗性的解释。报告的体例由刘斌设计，主要章节由刘斌和王宁远撰写，赵晔、陈明辉、闫凯凯、王永磊、朱叶菲、宋姝、朱雪菲、武欣等也撰写了部分章节，最后由刘斌统一修改审定。在每个章节的标题后面都注明了撰写人姓名。李永加完成了四面城墙、美人地、姜家山、钟家港等遗址出土遗物的拍摄工作，同时还选用了吴正龙拍摄的反山、瑶山部分玉器的照片，陈明辉、闫凯凯在报告的配图方面做了大量工作，陈明辉、朱叶菲、宋姝、武欣对报告进行了仔细核校。

良渚遗址的考古与保护，经历了几十年的艰苦奋斗和不断探索，能够取得今天的成绩，这其中凝结了几代考古人的汗水和心血。每一次有重要的考古发现，我们都会积极呼吁保护，并得到国家文物局、浙江省文物局以及各级政府领导的大力支持。如1986年反山遗址发掘后不久，就成立了良渚遗址管理所；瑶山遗址发掘完，就对整个祭坛进行了砌石保护；1987年，在配合老104国道拓宽工程的考古发掘中发现莫角山遗址，该工程也随之取消，而在遗址外围新建了新104国道；汇观山祭坛也做了很好的保护展示。正是这一步一步不断发现，不断保护，从点到面，最终有了一个保存基本完整、认识基本全面的良渚古代王国。

这一路走来，我们也得到了许多像张忠培先生、严文明先生这样的老一辈考古学家们关心和指导，得到了当地老百姓的理解、帮助与牺牲奉献。当我们站在历史的光环下，感受五千年中华文明辉煌的时候，我们的内心充满了感激！感恩这所有的一切！

考古队主要人员有：

刘斌、王宁远、赵晔、闫凯凯、陈明辉、王永磊、朱雪菲、姬翔、朱叶菲、宋姝、武欣、汪飞英、祁自立、芦西燕、陈全合、徐有秀、王可兴、郭留通、孟小玲、王新民、江稚平、孙小政、史全兴、刘晓伟。

勘探队主要人员有：

王琳、杨云、李西海、雷博、王贵军、李强强、雷文昌、高恩社、赵刚义、郝鹏飞、葛林、薛春民、刘云仓、刘占龙。

刘斌

2018年4月于八角亭良渚工作站

# A Comprehensive Study of Liangzhu Ancient City

(Abstract)

The Liangzhu Civilization (3300 BC-2300 BC) represents the peak of prehistoric cultures in the Taihu Lake region of the Lower Yangtze River. Covering at enormous area of around  $36,500\mathrm{km}^2$ , its core distributional area is situated between  $30^{\circ}$  and  $32^{\circ}$  North latitude, and  $119^{\circ}10^{\prime \prime}$  and  $121^{\circ}55^{\prime \prime}$  East longitude. This places it within the same latitudinal zone as the other contemporary ancient civilizations of the Old World. The developmental trajectory of prehistoric archaeological cultures around the Taihu Lake太湖 is relatively clear, thanks to the numerous archaeological excavations and research. Beginning in 5000 BC and lasting until 2000 BC, this region witnessed the rise and fall of the Majiabang 马家浜 Culture, Songze 森泽 Culture, Liangzhu Culture, Qianshanyang 钱山漾 Culture, and Guangfuling 广富林 Culture.

There are more than 600 Liangzhu Culture sites that have been found in the area surrounding the Taihu Lake. Liangzhu Ancient City 良渚古城 is the biggest one with earthen walls and complex structure, and can therefore be considered as the urban center of the Liangzhu Civilization. The Liangzhu Ancient City is located in the Yuhang 余杭 District of Zhejiang 浙江 Province, southeast China, seated in the northern part of a C-shaped basin measuring  $1,000 \mathrm{~km}^2$  in area. Towering over this basin are the Dazheshan Mountain 大遮山 to the south and the Dahongshan Mountain 大雄山 to the north, while the west of the basin is bordered by a range of foothills. Benefiting from its fertile soils and rich natural resources, the basin provide very advantageous natural condition for the growth and expansion of the prehistoric city.

The core of the Liangzhu Ancient City is divided into three enclosures. In the center is the Mojiaoshan 莫角山 Palace area of 30 ha, surrounded by the inner walls and outer walls, which enclose areas of 300 ha and 630 ha, respectively. The heights of the architecture on the artificial mounds gradually descend from the interior to the exterior of the city. Such topographic difference is likely indicative of the social status differences of its residents. In addition to this, we have discovered an enormous Water Conservancy System located to the north and west of the city, as well as two sacrificial altars at the Yaoshan 瑶山 and Huiguanshan 汇观山 Site. The outskirts of the walled city also featured a vast suburban district. In total, these features occupied a vast area of around  $100\mathrm{km}^2$ . Radiocarbon dates show that the Liangzhu Ancient City came into use around 3100 BC.

All these sites were built atop artificial mounds and the construction of them must have required labor and resource demanding. According to our rough estimation, construction of the city and its Water Conservancy System

would have required the removal of earth about  $10,050,000\mathrm{m}^3$ . Such enormous scale of construction activities required the mobilization of a huge amount of human labor and probably took several decades to complete.

The Liangzhu Ancient City has a rectangular shape with round corners and is situated along a north-south axis. It is  $1,910\mathrm{m}$  long north-to-south,  $1,770\mathrm{m}$  wide east-to-west, covering a total area of nearly  $3\mathrm{km}$ . The city wall is approximately  $6\mathrm{km}$  long,  $20\mathrm{m}$  to  $150\mathrm{m}$  wide, and  $4\mathrm{m}$  high at the best preserved sections. A layer of stone foundation, about  $20\mathrm{cm}$  to  $40\mathrm{cm}$  thick, is laid at the bottom to reinforce the rammed earthen wall made with yellow earth collected from nearby hills. All sides of the city walls are sandwiched between an inner and an outer moat, except for the southern side where the outer moat is absent. Eight water gates connecting the inner and outer moats have been found through coring surveys, two on each of the four sides of the city walls. The ground surface along the southern wall is higher and no outer moat has been found there, but an overland gate is located in the central section of the southern wall.

Many artificial earthen mounds were built throughout the city, mainly used as foundations for buildings and cemeteries. Mojiaoshan, the palace district inside the city, sits on such an earthen mound in the centre of the city. The Fanshan 反山 elite cemetery, where eleven elite burials with an abundance of Liangzhu jade objects were excavated in 1986, is situated right next to the northwest corner of Mojiaoshan. In addition to the moats along the city wall, there are also 51 canals and rivers inside the city, and the total length of the city's canals adds up to more than  $30\mathrm{km}$ .

Mojiaoshan Palace area, which is located in the centre of the Liangzhu Ancient City, is an artificially constructed earthen mound with a rectangular shape. The base of the platform is  $630\mathrm{m}$  by  $450\mathrm{m}$ , occupying an area of nearly  $300,000\mathrm{m}$ . Our estimation suggests that  $2,280,000\mathrm{m}^3$  of earth would have been moved to build the mound. There are three palatial platforms in the Mojiaoshan Palace area, namely Damojiaoshan 大莫角山, Xiaomojiaoshan 小莫角山, and Wuguishan 乌龟山. We discovered seven foundations for the palatial architecture arranged in two rows on top of Damojiaoshan, each occupying an area of about  $300\mathrm{m}$  to  $900\mathrm{m}$ . Similarly, four foundations for the palatial architecture were found on top of Xiaomojiaoshan. A rammed earth and sand square was widely distributed in the center of Mojiaoshan, covering an area over  $70,000\mathrm{m}$  in total. The square was an important area for major activities taken place on top of the Mojiaoshan. Excavation of the eastern slope of Mojiaoshan shows that the yellowish clay layer was built gradually with patches and ridges of clay, identical to the construction technology applied in the south city wall that was also built by stacking grass-wrapped silt. Also, the excavated Pit No. H11 contained  $13,000\mathrm{kg}$  of carbonised rice.

There are long strips of artificially constructed mounds in the outer circle of the Liangzhu Archaeological Site, including Biandanshan 扁担山—Heshangdi 和尚地, Lishan 里山—Zhengcun 郑村—Gaocun 高村, Bianjiashan 卞家山, Dongyangjiacun 东杨家村, and Xiyangjiacun 西杨家村, each about  $30 \mathrm{~m}$  to  $60 \mathrm{~m}$  wide, with a height of about  $1 \mathrm{~m}$  to  $3 \mathrm{~m}$ , occupying an area of  $6.3 \mathrm{~km}^{2}$ . Excavations showed that Meirendi 美人地, Lishan and Biandanshan are raised residential areas that were built up during the Late Liangzhu Period. Remains of the wooden riverbank on the edge of the Meirendi earthen platform provide a vivid picture of waterside life around 4500 years ago. Cemeteries, canals and docks from the Early Liangzhu Period to Late Liangzhu Period are

discovered at the Bianjiashan Site. An cemetery from the Early Liangzhu Period to Late Liangzhu Period was also found at Wenjiashan 文家山。

A large water conservancy system located to the northwest of the Liangzhu Ancient City has been discovered through coring surveys and test excavations in recent years. The dams discovered so far are located in front of the valleys formed by two hills and can be divided into a north and a south group. Low dam system including Tangshan 塘山, Shizishan 狮子山, Liyushan 鲤鱼山, Guanshan 官山 and Wutongnong 梧桐弄 make up the south group, while high dam system in the north include Ganggongling 岗公岭, Laohuling 老虎岭, Zhoujiafan 周家畈, Qiuwu 秋坞, Shiwu 石坞, and Mifengnong 蜜蜂弄. These dams created a protective system with two lines of barriers. The reservoirs of this hydraulic system located occupies an area of about  $13\mathrm{km}^2$  and has a capacity of water storage about 50,000,000  $\mathrm{m}^3$ , based on our computational simulation. The structure and function of this hydraulic system is not yet fully understood as systematic investigation has only just begun. Based on our field observations, we think the function of this hydraulic system includes flood prevention, transport, water storage, and irrigation.

Altars and elite cemeteries such as Yaoshan瑶山 and Huiguanshan 汇观山 are located outside the city wall of the Liangzhu Ancient City. Yaoshan is a natural hill  $35\mathrm{m}$  above sea level, located  $5\mathrm{km}$  northeast of the Liangzhu Ancient City. There are slope protection structures made of stone on the west and north sides of the altar. The top of the altar is flat. The construction procedure of the platform is as follows. Trenches were dug out and earth was compacted to form a concentric square structure with a red earthen platform inside, a square of trenches filled with greyish earth in the middle, and a gravel platform on the outside. There are thirteen elaborate Liangzhu burials distributed in two rows south of the altar with their graves cutting into the altar.

Outside of Liangzhu Ancient City's core area of  $6.3\mathrm{km}^2$ , within the city system limits of  $100\mathrm{km}^2$ , is the vast rural areas. There is a rural area of  $25\mathrm{km}^2$  to the east and north of the city, and previous investigation has revealed that there are more 80 sites, including center villages, small-sized villages, altars, cemeteries, handicraft workshops, rice paddy fields and so on. In recent years, we have been conducting extensive and systematic coring surveys in the eastern rural area outside the city. We completed an area of  $3.6\mathrm{km}^2$  in 2015-2017 and found 97 sites, the number of sites in this area we known is only about 20. It can be surmised that the occupation density of this rural area is far beyond the original investigation.

The Liangzhu hinterland—the C-shaped basin of about  $1,000\mathrm{km}^2$  is also occupied by many Liangzhu Culture sites. The most important area is Linping临平 site group with nearly 20 sites, about  $30\mathrm{km}$  east of the Liangzhu Ancient City, including Hengshan 横山 elite cemetery, the Maoshan 茅山 rice paddy fields and the Yujiashan 玉架山 moated settlement.
